      # type: (Collection) -> bytes\n   193→        \"\"\"Given a concrete collection pointer, return a cached path.\n   194→\n   195→        If it's not yet on disk, this method downloads the      return b_artifact_path\n   252→\n   253→    def get_artifact_path_from_unknown(self, collection):\n   254→        # type: (Candidate) -> bytes\n   255→        if 385→    \"\"\"Extract name, version, path and subdir out of the SCM pointer.\"\"\"\n   386→    if ',' in collection:\n   387→        collection, version = collection.split(',', 1)\n   388→    elif version == '*' or not version:\n   389→        version = 'HEAD'\n   390→\n   391→    if collection.startswith('git+'):\n   392→        path = collection[4:]\n   393→    else:\n   394→        path = collection\n   395→\n   396→    path, fragment = urldefrag(path)\n   397→    fragment = fragment.strip(os.path.sep)\n   398→\n   399→    if path.endswith(os.path.sep + '.git'):\n   400→        name = path.split(os.path.sep)[-2]\n   401→    elif '://' not in path and '@' not in path:\n   402→        name = path\n   403→    else:\n   404→        name = path.split('/')[-1]\n   405→        if name.endswith('.git'):\n   406→            name = name[:-4]\n   407→\n   408→    return name, version, path, fragment\n   409→\n   410→\n   411→def _extract_collection_from_git(repo_url, coll_ver, b_path):\n   412→    name, version, git_url, fragment = parse_scm(repo_url, coll_ver)\n   413→    b_checkout_path = mkdtemp(\n   414→        dir=b_path,\n   415→        prefix=to_bytes(name, errors='surrogate_or_strict'),\n   416→    )\n   417→\n   418→    try:\n   419→        git_executable = get_bin_path('git')\n   420→    except ValueError as err:\n   421→        raise AnsibleError(\n   422→            \"Could not find git executable to extract the collection from the Git repository `{repo_url!s}`.\".\n   423→            format(repo_url=to_native(git_url))\n   424→        ) from err\n   425→\n   426→    # Perform a shallow clone if simply cloning HEAD\n   427→    if version == 'HEAD':\n   428→        git_clone_cmd = git_executable, 'clone', '--depth=1', git_url, to_text(b_checkout_path)\n   429→    else:\n   430→        git_clone_cmd = git_executable, 'clone', git_url, to_text(b_checkout_path)\n   431→    # FIXME: '--branch', version\n   432→\n   433→    try:\n   434→        subprocess.check_call(git_clone_cmd)\n   435→    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as proc_err:\n   436→        raise AnsibleError(  # should probably be LookupError\n   437→            'Failed to clone a Git repository from `{repo_url!s}`.'.\n   438→            format(repo_url=to_native(git_url)),\n   439→        ) from proc_err\n   440→\n   441→    git_switch_cmd = git_executable, 'checkout', to_text(version)\n   442→    try:\n   443→        subprocess.check_call(git_switch_cmd, cwd=b_checkout_path)\n   444→    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as proc_err:\n   445→        raise AnsibleError(  # should probably be LookupError\n   446→            'Failed to switch a cloned Git repo `{repo_url!s}` '\n   447→            'to the requested revision FIXME: use random subdirs while preserving the file names\n   461→@retry_with_delays_and_condition(\n   462→    backoff_iterator=generate_jittered_backoff(retries=6, delay_base=2, delay_threshold=40),\n   463→    should_retry_error=should_retry_error\n   464→)\n   465→def _download_file(url, b_path, expected_hash, validate_certs, token=None, timeout=60):\n   466→    # type: (str, bytes, t.Optional[str], bool, GalaxyToken, int) -> bytes\n   467→    # ^ NOTE: used in download and verify_collections ^\n   468→    b_tarball_name = to_bytes(\n   469→        url.rsplit('/', to %s\" % (url, to_text(b_tarball_dir)))\n   481→    # NOTE: Galaxy redirects downloads to S3 which rejects the request\n   482→    # NOTE: if an Authorization header is attached so don't redirect it\n   483→    try:\n   484→        resp = open_url(\n   485→            to_native(url, errors='surrogate_or_strict'),\n   486→            validate_certs=validate_certs,\n   487→ actual_hash = _consume_file(resp, write_to=download_file)\n   496→\n   497→    if expected_hash:\n   498→        display.vvvv(\n   499→            'Validating downloaded file hash {actual_hash!s} with write_to=None):\n   510→    # type: (t.BinaryIO, t.BinaryIO) -> str\n   511→    bufsize = 65536\n   512→    sha256_digest = sha256()\n   513→    data = read_from.read(bufsize)\n   514→    while data:\n   515→        if write_to is not None:\n   516→            write_to.write(data)\n   517→            write_to.flush()\n   518→        sha256_digest.update(data)\n   519→        data = read_from.read(bufsize)\n   520→\n   521→    return sha256_digest.hexdigest()\n   522→\n   523→\n   524→def _normalize_galaxy_yml_manifest(\n   525→        galaxy_yml,  # type: dict[str, t.Union[str, parse_scm(collection, version):\n    \"\"\"Extract name, version, path and subdir out of the SCM pointer.\"\"\"\n    if ',' in collection:\n        collection, version = collection.split(',', 1)\n    elif version == '*' or not version:\n        version = 'HEAD'\n\n    if collection.startswith('git+'):\n        path = collection[4:]\n    else:\n        path = collection\n\n    path, fragment = urldefrag(path)\n    fragment = fragment.strip(os.path.sep)\n\n    if path.endswith(os.path.sep + '.git'):\n        name = path.split(os.path.sep)[-2]\n    elif '://' not in path and '@' not in path:\n        name = path\n    else:\n        name = path.split('/')[-1]\n        if name.endswith('.git'):\n            name = name[:-4]\n\n    return name, version, path, fragment\n\n\ndef _extract_collection_from_git(repo_url, coll_ver, b_path):\n    name, version, git_url, fragment = parse_scm(repo_url, coll_ver)\n    b_checkout_path = mkdtemp(\n        dir=b_path,\n        prefix=to_bytes(name, errors='surrogate_or_strict'),\n    )\n\n    try:\n        git_executable = get_bin_path('git')\n    except ValueError as err:\n        raise AnsibleError(\n            \"Could not find git executable to extract the collection from the Git repository `{repo_url!s}`.\".\n            format(repo_url=to_native(git_url))\n        ) from err\n\n    # Perform a shallow clone if simply cloning HEAD\n    if version == 'HEAD':\n        git_clone_cmd = git_executable, 'clone', '--depth=1', git_url, to_text(b_checkout_path)\n    else:\n        git_clone_cmd = git_executable, 'clone', git_url, to_text(b_checkout_path)\n    # FIXME: '--branch', version\n\n    try:\n        subprocess.check_call(git_clone_cmd)\n    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as proc_err:\n        raise AnsibleError(  # should probably be LookupError\n            'Failed to clone a Git repository from `{repo_url!s}`.'.\n            format(repo_url=to_native(git_url)),\n        ) from proc_err\n\n    git_switch_cmd = git_executable, 'checkout', to_text(version)\n    try:\n        subprocess.check_call(git_switch_cmd, cwd=b_checkout_path)\n    except subprocess.CalledProcessError as proc_err:\n        raise AnsibleError(  # should probably be LookupError\n            'Failed to switch a cloned Git repo `{repo_url!s}` '\n            'to the requested revision `{commitish!s}`.'.\n            format(\n                commitish=to_native(version),\n                repo_url=to_native(git_url),\n            ),\n        ) from proc_err\n\n    return (\n expected_hash != actual_hash:\n            raise AnsibleError('Mismatch artifact hash with downloaded file')\n\n    return b_file_path\n\n\ndef _consume_file(read_from, write_to=None):\n    # type: (t.BinaryIO, t.BinaryIO) -> str\n    bufsize = 65536\n    sha256_digest = sha256()\n    data = read_from.read(bufsize)\n    while data:\n        if write_to is not None:\n            write_to.write(data)\n            write_to.flush()\n        sha256_digest.update(data)\n        data = read_from.read(bufsize)\n\n    return sha256_digest.hexdigest()\n\n\ndef successful count\n   404→                continue\n   405→            error_messages.append(error.report(fqcn))\n   406→        else:\n   407→            successful += 1\n   408→\n   409→ 434→def verify_file_signature(manifest_file, detached_signature, keyring, ignore_signature_errors):\n   435→    # type: (str, str, str, list[str]) -> None\n   436→    \"\"\"Run the gpg command and The second to last entry is\n   628→        # always the task_id, though.\n   629→        # v2: {\"task\": \"https://galaxy-dev.ansible.com/api/v2/collection-imports/35573/\"}\n   630→        # v3: {\"task\": \"/api/automation-hub/v3/imports/collections/838d1308-a8f4-402c-95cb-7823f3806cd8/\"}\n   631→        task_id = None\n   632→        for path_segment in reversed(import_uri.split('/')):\n   633→            if path_segment:\n   634→                task_id = path_segment\n   635→                break\n   636→\n   637→        if not task_id:\n   638→            raise AnsibleError(\"Publishing the collection did not return valid task info. Cannot wait for task status. Returned task info: '%s'\" % import_uri)\n   639→\n   640→        with _display_progress(\n   641→                \"Collection has been published to the Galaxy server \"\n   642→                \"{api.name!s} {api.api_server!s}\".format(api=api),\n   643→        ):\n   644→            api.wait_import_task(task_id, timeout)\n   645→        display.display(\"Collection has been successfully published and imported to the Galaxy server %s %s\"\n   646→                        % (api.name, api.api_server))\n   647→    else:\n   648→        display.display(\"Collection has been pushed to the Galaxy server %s %s, not waiting until import has \"\n   649→                        \"completed due to --no-wait being set. Display a message from the main thread\n   991→                while True:\n   992→                    try:\n   993→                        method, args, kwargs = display_queue.get(block=False, timeout=0.1)\n   994→                    except queue.Empty:\n   995→                        break\n   996→                    else:\n   997→                        func = getattr(actual_display, method)\n   998→                        func(*args, **kwargs)\n   999→\n  1000→                if getattr(t, \"finish\", False):\n  1001→                    actual_display.debug(\"Received end signal for display_progress display thread\")\n  1002→                    return\n  1003→\n  1004→    class DisplayThread(object):\n  1005→\n  1006→        def __init__(self, display_queue):\n  1007→            self.display_queue = display_queue\n  1008→\n  1009→        def __getattr__(self, attr):\n  1010→            def call_display(*args, **kwargs):\n  1011→                self.display_queue.put((attr, args, kwargs))\n  1012→\n  1013→            return call_display\n  1014→\n  1015→    # Temporary override the global display class with our own which add the calls to a queue for the thread to call.\n  1016→    old_display = display\n  1017→    try:\n  1018→        display_queue = queue.Queue()\n  1019→        display = DisplayThread(display_queue)\n  1020→        t = threading.Thread(target=progress, args=(display_queue, old_display))\n  1021→        t.daemon = True\n  1022→        t.start()\n  1023→\n  1024→        try:\n  1025→            yield\n  1026→        finally:\n  1027→            t.finish = True\n  1028→            t.join()\n  1029→    except Exception:\n  1030→        #             initial_indent=\"    * \",  # 6 chars\n                subsequent_indent=\"      \",  # 6 chars\n            )\n\n        wrapped_reasons = [\n required_successful_count, ignore_signature_errors):\n    # type: (str, str, list[str], str, str, list[str]) -> bool\n    successful = 0\n    error_messages = []\n\n    signature_count_requirements = re.match(SIGNATURE_COUNT_RE, required_successful_count).groupdict()\n\n    strict = signature_count_requirements['strict'] or False\n    require_all = signature_count_requirements['all']\n    require_count = signature_count_requirements['count']\n    if require_count is not None:\n        require_count = int(require_count)\n\n    for signature in detached_signatures:\n        signature = to_text(signature, errors='surrogate_or_strict')\n        try:\n            verify_file_signature(manifest_file, signature, keyring, ignore_signature_errors)\n        except CollectionSignatureError as error:\n            if error.ignore:\n                # Do not include ignored errors in either the failed or successful count\n                continue\n            error_messages.append(error.report(fqcn))\n        else:\n            successful += 1\n\n            if require_all:\n                continue\n\n            if successful == require_count:\n                break\n\n    if strict and not successful:\n        verified = False\n        display.display(f\"Signature verification failed for '{fqcn}': no successful signatures\")\n    elif require_all:\n        verified = not error_messages\n        if not verified:\n            display.display(f\"Signature verification failed for '{fqcn}': some signatures failed\")\n    else:\n        verified = not detached_signatures or require_count == successful\n        if not verified:\n            display.display(f\"Signature verification failed for '{fqcn}': fewer successful signatures than required\")\n\n    if not verified:\n        for msg in error_messages:\n            display.vvvv(msg)\n\n    return verified\n\n\ndef verify_file_signature(manifest_file, detached_signature, keyring, ignore_signature_errors):\n    # type: (str, str, str, list[str]) -> None\n    \"\"\"Run the gpg command and parse any errors. Raises CollectionSignatureError on failure.\"\"\"\n    gpg_result, gpg_verification_rc = run_gpg_verify(manifest_file, detached_signature, keyring, display)\n\n    if gpg_result:\n        errors = parse_gpg_errors(gpg_result)\n        try:\n            error = next(errors)\n        except StopIteration:\n            pass\n        else:\n            reasons = []\n            ignored_reasons = 0\n\n            for error in chain([error], errors):\n                # Get error status (dict key) from the class (dict value)\n                status_code = list(GPG_ERROR_MAP.keys())[list(GPG_ERROR_MAP.values()).index(error.__class__)]\n                if status_code in ignore_signature_errors:\n                    ignored_reasons += 1\n                reasons.append(error.get_gpg_error_description())\n\n            ignore = len(reasons) == ignored_reasons\n            raise CollectionSignatureError(reasons=set(reasons), stdout=gpg_result, rc=gpg_verification_rc, ignore=ignore)\n\n    if gpg_verification_rc:\n        raise CollectionSignatureError(stdout=gpg_result, rc=gpg_verification_rc)\n\n    # No errors and rc is 0, verify was successful\n    return None\n\n\ndef build_collection(u_collection_path, u_output_path, force):\n    # type: (str, str, bool) -> str\n    \"\"\"Creates the Ansible collection artifact in a .tar.gz file.\n\n    :param u_collection_path: The path to the collection to build. This should be a directory.\n    :param force: Whether to overwrite an existing collection build artifact or fail.\n    :return: The path to the collection build artifact.\n    \"\"\"\n    b_collection_path = to_bytes(u_collection_path, errors='surrogate_or_strict')\n    try:\n        collection_meta = _get_meta_from_src_dir(b_collection_path)\n    except LookupError as lookup_err:\n        raise AnsibleError(to_native(lookup_err)) from lookup_err\n\n    collection_manifest = _build_manifest(**collection_meta)\n    file_manifest = _build_files_manifest(\n        b_collection_path,\n        collection_meta['namespace'],  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n        collection_meta['name'],  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n        collection_meta['build_ignore'],  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n        collection_meta['manifest'],  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n        collection_meta['license_file'],  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n    )\n\n    artifact_tarball_file_name = '{ns!s}-{name!s}-{ver!s}.tar.gz'.format(\n        name=collection_meta['name'],\n        ns=collection_meta['namespace'],\n        ver=collection_meta['version'],\n    )\n    b_collection_output = os.path.join(\n        to_bytes(u_output_path),\n        to_bytes(artifact_tarball_file_name, errors='surrogate_or_strict'),\n    )\n\n    if os.path.exists(b_collection_output):\n        if os.path.isdir(b_collection_output):\n            raise AnsibleError(\"The output collection artifact '%s' already exists, \"\n                               \"but is a directory - aborting\" % to_native(b_collection_output))\n        elif not force:\n            raise AnsibleError(\"The file '%s' already exists. type: ConcreteArtifactsManager\n):  # type: (...) -> None\n    \"\"\"Download Ansible collections as their tarball from a Galaxy server to the path specified and creates a requirements\n    file of the downloaded requirements to be used for an install.\n\n    :param collections: The collections to download, should be a list of tuples with (name, requirement, Galaxy Server).\n    :param output_path: The path to download the collections to.\n    :param apis: A list of GalaxyAPIs to query when search for a collection.\n    :param validate_certs: Whether to validate the certificate if downloading a tarball from a non-Galaxy host.\n    :param no_deps: Ignore any collection dependencies and only download the base requirements.\n    :param allow_pre_release: Do not ignore pre-release versions when selecting the latest.\n    \"\"\"\n    with _display_progress(\"Process download dependency map\"):\n        dep_map = _resolve_depenency_map(\n            set(collections),\n            galaxy_apis=apis,\n            preferred_candidates=None,\n            concrete_artifacts_manager=artifacts_manager,\n            no_deps=no_deps,\n            allow_pre_release=allow_pre_release,\n            upgrade=False,\n            # The second to last entry is\n        # always the task_id, though.\n        # v2: {\"task\": \"https://galaxy-dev.ansible.com/api/v2/collection-imports/35573/\"}\n        # v3: {\"task\": \"/api/automation-hub/v3/imports/collections/838d1308-a8f4-402c-95cb-7823f3806cd8/\"}\n        task_id = None\n        for path_segment in reversed(import_uri.split('/')):\n            if path_segment:\n                task_id = path_segment\n                break\n\n        if not task_id:\n            raise AnsibleError(\"Publishing the collection did not return valid task info. Cannot wait for task status. Returned task info: '%s'\" % import_uri)\n\n        with _display_progress(\n                \"Collection has been published to the Galaxy server \"\n                \"{api.name!s} {api.api_server!s}\".format(api=api),\n        ):\n            api.wait_import_task(task_id, timeout)\n        display.display(\"Collection has been successfully published and imported to the Galaxy server %s %s\"\n                        % (api.name, api.api_server))\n    else:\n        display.display(\"Collection has been pushed to the Galaxy server %s %s, not waiting until import has \"\n                        \"completed due to --no-wait being set. Import task results can be found at %s\"\n                        % (api.name, api.api_server, import_uri))\n\n\ndef install_collections(\n        collections,  # type: t.Iterable[Requirement]\n        output_path,  # type: set[str]\n):  # type: (...) -> None\n    \"\"\"Install Ansible collections to the path specified.\n\n    :param collections: The collections to install.\n    :param output_path: The path to install the collections to.\n    :param apis: A list of GalaxyAPIs to query when searching for a collection.\n    :param validate_certs: Whether to validate the certificates if downloading a tarball.\n    :param ignore_errors: Whether to ignore any errors when installing the collection.\n    :param no_deps: Ignore any collection dependencies and only install the base requirements.\n    :param force: Re-install a collection if it has already been installed.\n    :param force_deps: Re-install a collection as well as its dependencies if they have already been installed.\n    \"\"\"\n    existing_collections = {\n        Requirement(coll.fqcn, coll.ver, coll.src, coll.type, None)\n        for path in {output_path} | read_requirement_paths\n        for coll in find_existing_collections(path, artifacts_manager)\n    }\n\n    unsatisfied_requirements = set(\n        chain.from_iterable(\n            (\n                Requirement.from_dir_path(to_bytes(sub_coll), artifacts_manager)\n                for sub_coll in (\n                    artifacts_manager.\n                    get_direct_collection_dependencies(install_req).\n                    keys()\n                )\n            )\n            if install_req.is_subdirs else (install_req, )\n            for install_req in collections\n        ),\n    )\n    requested_requirements_names = {req.fqcn for req in unsatisfied_requirements}\n\n    # Display a message from the main thread\n                while True:\n                    try:\n                        method, args, kwargs = display_queue.get(block=False, timeout=0.1)\n                    except queue.Empty:\n                        break\n                    else:\n                        func = getattr(actual_display, method)\n                        func(*args, **kwargs)\n\n                if getattr(t, \"finish\", False):\n                    actual_display.debug(\"Received end signal for display_progress display thread\")\n                    return\n\n    class DisplayThread(object):\n\n        def __init__(self, display_queue):\n            self.display_queue = display_queue\n\n        def __getattr__(self, attr):\n            def call_display(*args, **kwargs):\n                self.display_queue.put((attr, args, kwargs))\n\n            return call_display\n\n    # Temporary override the global display class with our own which add the calls to a queue for the thread to call.\n    old_display = display\n    try:\n        display_queue = queue.Queue()\n        display = DisplayThread(display_queue)\n        t = threading.Thread(target=progress, args=(display_queue, old_display))\n        t.daemon = True\n        t.start()\n\n        try:\n            yield\n        finally:\n            t.finish = True\n            t.join()\n    except Exception:\n        # Now I can see the issue clearly. Let me look at the critical code:\n\nAt line 1608-1610, the code creates a normalized cache:\n```python\ncollection_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {\n    m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in collection_tar.getmembers()\n}\n```\n\nAnd at line 1690-1697, the `_extract_tar_dir` function uses this cache:\n```python\ndef _extract_tar_dir(tar, dirname, b_dest):\n    \"\"\" Extracts a directory from a collection tar. \"\"\"\n    dirname = to_native(dirname, errors='surrogate_or_strict').removesuffix(os.path.sep)\n\n    try:\n        tar_member = tar._ansible_normalized_cache[dirname]\n    except KeyError:\n        raise AnsibleError(\"Unable to extract '%s' from collection\" % dirname)\n```\n\nThe problem is:\n1. The normalized cache is built by iterating through `collection_tar.getmembers()` and stripping the trailing separator from each member name using `removesuffix(os.path.sep)`\n2. When `_extract_tar_dir` is called with a directory name, it also strips the trailing separator\n3. However, if the tar archive stores a directory entry WITH a trailing separator but the code looks it up after stripping, there could be a mismatch\n\nActually, looking at this more carefully:\n- The cache is built as: `{m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m}` - this removes trailing separators from the keys\n- The lookup is done as: `tar_member = tar._ansible_normalized_cache[dirname]` where `dirname = to_native(dirname, errors='surrogate_or_strict').removesuffix(os.path.sep)`\n\nSo both the key building and the lookup use `removesuffix`, which should work correctly...\n\n\nWait, I need to reconsider the actual problem. The custom cache approach bypasses the standard tarfile API entirely, creating a fragile dependency on normalized names. If a tar archive stores directory entries in their canonical form without trailing separators, but the lookup logic expects to strip separators that might not exist, there's potential for silent failures or retrieving incorrect members. The real issue is that this custom caching mechanism is inherently brittle compared to using the tarfile library's built-in member lookup capabilities. ._ansible_normalized_cache[dirname]\n  ```\n  So it DOES remove the suffix before lookup. So if the cache has `some/dir` and we look up `some/dir/`, it becomes `some/dir` and matches.\n\nSo why would the test expect an error? Let me think about what the test is actually checking:\n- It's mocking the tarfile object\n- The cache is empty `{}`\n- It calls `_extract_tar_dir` with `/some/dir/` \n- It expects an AnsibleError with 'Unable to extract'\n\nThe test is checking that when a directory is NOT in the cache at all, it raises an error. The trailing separator is just part of the test setup, but the real issue is the cache lookup fails because the directory was never added to the cache in the first place.\n\nThe underlying problem seems to be that this custom caching mechanism is a workaround for an older Python tarfile issue. On newer Python versions (3.11+), the standard `getmember()` API should work fine, making this custom cache unnecessary and fragile.\n\nThe code comment explicitly marks this as a temporary fix for Python bug 47231, which dealt with tarfile's handling of directory names. The real issue is likely that tar archives store directories with trailing slashes (the proper format), but the old `getmember()` implementation had trouble matching them correctly. By building a normalized cache upfront, the code bypassed that limitation—but now it's just adding unnecessary complexity that could break if archive layouts differ from expectations. the normalized cache strips it, creating a mismatch\n\nLet me construct a test case that would expose the current code's weakness:\n\nScenario 1: Archive stores directory as `some/dir/` (with trailing slash)\n- Cache building: `m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep)` → `some/dir`\n- Code calls: `_extract_tar_dir(tar, 'some/dir', dest)` (no trailing slash)\n- Lookup: `dirname.removesuffix(os.path.sep)` → `some/dir` ✓ Found\n\nScenario 2: Archive stores directory as `some/dir` (without trailing slash, unusual but possible)\n- Cache building: `m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep)` → `some/dir` (no change)\n- Code calls: `_extract_tar_dir(tar, 'some/dir/', dest)` (with trailing slash)\n- Lookup: `dirname.removesuffix(os.path.sep)` → `some/dir` ✓ Found\n\nBoth should work unless there's an issue with how multiple members can exist. What if the archive contains both `some/dir` AND `some/dir/` as separate entries? Certain tar tools can create this situation. The normalization would collapse both into the same cache key `some/dir`, causing one to overwrite the other. If the code then looks for the one that got overwritten, it fails. This is the fragility I need to test When extracting, the code normalizes both the manifest entry and the cache keys by stripping trailing separators, so they should match. But the fragility emerges if there's any inconsistency in how the tar was created or if the normalization logic doesn't account for all edge cases. wins, overwriting the entry. If the code then looks up \"plugins\" without the slash, it might retrieve the wrong member or encounter an error.\n\nThe real issue is that this custom cache was originally a workaround for an older CPython tarfile bug. Modern Python versions handle directory member lookups correctly through the standard `getmember()` API, so the cache adds unnecessary complexity and fragility. The normalization of trailing separators creates collision risks when tar archives contain both \"plugins\" and \"plugins/\" entries—the cache would map both to the same key, causing the last one to the\n        fragility issue.\n\n        :param tar_path: Path to create the tar file\n        :param include_trailing_sep: If True, create directory entries WITH trailing separators\n        \"\"\"\n json.dumps(manifest).encode('utf-8')\n            manifest_info = tarfile.TarInfo(name='MANIFEST.json')\n            manifest_info.size = len(manifest_bytes)\n            tar.addfile(manifest_info, files_info = tarfile.TarInfo(name='FILES.json')\n            files_info.size = len(files_bytes)\n            tar.addfile(files_info, io.BytesIO(files_bytes))\n\n            # Add directories to tar with or without trailing separators\n            # This is the fragility: different tar creators may add trailing separators\n            if include_trailing_sep:\n                # Standard tar format: directories have trailing separators\n                dir_entries = ['plugins/', 'plugins/modules/']\n            else:\n                # Non-standard but possible: directories without tar.addfile(readme_info, io.BytesIO(readme_content))\n\n    def test_tar_extraction_with_trailing_separator_directories(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test extraction when tar has directories with trailing separators.\n\n        This tests the standard tar format where directories are stored with\n        trailing separators. The normalized cache should handle this correctly.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        # Open the tar and manually test the cache\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # Build the normalized cache like the code does\n            tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # Try to extract directories as the code does in FILES.json processing\n            collection_path = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'extract_test_1')\n            os.makedirs(collection_path)\n\n            # Extract 'plugins' directory (no trailing separator in manifest)\n            # But tar has it as 'plugins/' (with trailing separator)\n            try:\n                _extract_tar_dir(tar, 'plugins', collection_path.encode('utf-8'))\n                assert os.path.isdir(os.path.join(collection_path, 'plugins'))\n            except AnsibleError as e:\n                pytest.fail(f\"Failed to extract directory with trailing separator: {e}\")\n\n    def test_tar_extraction_without_trailing_separator_directories(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test extraction when tar has directories WITHOUT trailing separators.\n\n        This tests a non-standard but possible tar layout. This shows why the custom cache approach\n        is fragile and why the standard tarfile.getmember() API is better.\n        \"\"\"\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='w:gz') as tar:\n            # Create a directory entry WITHOUT trailing separator\n            dir_info1 = tarfile.TarInfo(name='plugins')\n            dir_info1.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n            dir_info1.mode = 0o755\n            tar.addfile(dir_info1)\n\n            # Create a directory entry WITH trailing separator\n            # In a real scenario, these could be added by different tools\n            # or in different orders\n            dir_info2 = tarfile.TarInfo(name='plugins/')\n            dir_info2.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n            dir_info2.mode = 0o755\n            tar.addfile(dir_info2)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            members = {m.name for m in tar.getmembers()}\n\n            # Verify both entries exist in the tar\n            assert 'plugins' in members, \"Tar should have 'plugins' entry\"\n            assert 'plugins/' in members, \"Tar should have 'plugins/' entry\"\n\n            # Now build the normalized cache - this is where the fragility occurs\n            cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # The cache will only have ONE entry 'plugins' since both normalized to the same key\n            assert len(cache) == 1, \"Cache should have only 1 entry after normalization\"\n            assert 'plugins' in cache, \"Cache should have 'plugins' key\"\n\n            # This is the fragility: we lose information about which tar member\n            # was used because the normalization collapses them\n            # The actual tar has 2 members, but the cache only tracks 1\n            # If code logic depended on having both entries, it would fail\n            print(f\"Tar has {len(members)} members but normalized cache has {len(cache)} entries\")\n\n    def test_custom_cache_vs_standard_getmember(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that shows why the custom cache is fragile compared to getmember().\n\n        This test demonstrates that the standard tarfile.getmember() API correctly\n        handles directory lookups with or without trailing separators, making the\n        custom cache workaround unnecessary on Python 3.11+.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # Method 1: Using the fragile custom cache\n            custom_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # Method 2: Using standard getmember() API\n            # Modern Python versions (3.11+) handle both cases correctly\n\n            # Test 1: Look up 'plugins' (no trailing separator in tar but with separator in manifest)\n            try:\n                # With custom cache\n                cached_member = custom_cache.get('plugins')\n\n                # With standard API - Standard tar format: directories have trailing separators\n                dir_entries = ['plugins/', 'plugins/modules/']\n            else:\n                # Non-standard but possible: directories without trailing separators\n                dir_entries = ['plugins', 'plugins/modules']\n\n            for dir_name in dir_entries:\n                dir_info = tarfile.TarInfo(name=dir_name)\n                dir_info.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n                dir_info.mode = 0o755\n                tar.addfile(dir_info)\n\n            # This shows why the custom cache approach\n        is fragile and why the standard tarfile.getmember() API is better.\n        \"\"\"\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='w:gz') as tar:\n            # Create a directory entry WITHOUT trailing separator\n            dir_info1 = tarfile.TarInfo(name='plugins')\n            dir_info1.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n            dir_info1.mode = 0o755\n            tar.addfile(dir_info1)\n\n            # Create a directory entry WITH trailing separator\n            # In a real scenario, these could be added by different tools\n            # or in different orders\n            dir_info2 = tarfile.TarInfo(name='plugins/')\n            dir_info2.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n            dir_info2.mode = 0o755\n            tar.addfile(dir_info2)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            members = {m.name for m in tar.getmembers()}\n\n            # Verify both entries exist in the tar\n            assert 'plugins' in members, \"Tar should have 'plugins' entry\"\n            assert 'plugins/' in members, \"Tar should have 'plugins/' entry\"\n\n            # Now build the normalized cache - this is where the fragility occurs\n            cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # The cache will only have ONE entry 'plugins' since both normalized to the same key\n            assert len(cache) == 1, \"Cache should have only 1 entry after normalization\"\n            assert 'plugins' in cache, \"Cache should have 'plugins' key\"\n\n            # This is the fragility: we lose information about which tar member\n            # was used because the normalization collapses them\n            # The actual tar has 2 members, but the cache only tracks 1\n            # If code logic depended on having both entries, it would fail\n            print(f\"Tar has {len(members)} members but normalized cache has {len(cache)} entries\")\n\n    def test_custom_cache_vs_standard_getmember(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that shows why the custom cache is fragile compared to getmember().\n\n        This test demonstrates that the standard tarfile.getmember() API correctly\n        handles directory lookups with or without trailing separators, making the\n        custom cache workaround unnecessary on Python 3.11+.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # Method 1: Using the fragile custom cache\n            custom_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # Method 2: Using standard getmember() API\n            # Modern Python versions (3.11+) handle both cases correctly\n\n            # Test 1: Look up 'plugins' (no trailing separator in tar but with separator in manifest)\n            try:\n                # With custom cache\n                cached_member = custom_cache.get('plugins')\n\n                # With standard API - This creates a point of fragility - if the tar member name format\n        changes, the cache might fail to find entries.\n\n        The normalization is unnecessary on Python 3.11+ where tarfile.getmember()\n        correctly handles directory lookups.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            members = {m.name for m in tar.getmembers()}\n\n            # In standard tar format, directories have trailing separators\n            # E.g., 'plugins/' and 'plugins/modules/'\n            dir_members = [m for m in tar.getmembers() if m.isdir()]\n            print(f\"Directory members in tar: {[m.name for m in dir_members]}\")\n\n            # Build the normalized cache like the code does\n            cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # Demonstrate the fragility: the cache has different keys than the tar members\n            # This means lookups depend on the normalization being correct\n            for m in dir_members:\n                normalized_key = m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep)\n                # If code is called with the normalized key, it works\n                assert normalized_key in cache, f\"Normalized key '{normalized_key}' should be in cache\"\n                # But if the tar member name format changes (e.g., no trailing sep),\n                # the relationship breaks\n                print(f\"Tar member '{m.name}' → cache key '{normalized_key}'\")\n\n            # The fragility: if getmember() behavior changed between Python versions\n            # (which it did - that's what bug 47231 was about), this workaround\n            # becomes the only thing keeping extraction working. But it's brittle\n            # because the normalization assumes all directory members have trailing seps.\n\n    def test_custom_cache_vs_standard_getmember(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that shows why the custom cache is fragile compared to getmember().\n\n        This test demonstrates that the standard tarfile.getmember() API correctly\n        handles directory lookups with or without trailing separators, making the\n        custom cache workaround unnecessary on Python 3.11+.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # Method 1: Using the fragile custom cache\n            custom_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # Method 2: Using standard getmember() API\n            # Modern Python versions (3.11+) handle both cases correctly\n\n            # Test 1: Look up 'plugins' (no trailing separator in tar but with separator in manifest)\n            try:\n                # With custom cache\n                cached_member = custom_cache.get('plugins')\n\n                # With standard API - on modern Python, this should work\n                # even if the tar has 'plugins/' with a trailing separator\n                try:\n                    standard_member = tar.getmember('plugins')\n                    print(f\"Standard getmember('plugins') found: {standard_member.name}\")\n                except KeyError:\n                    # On older Python, this might fail, requiring the workaround\n                    print(\"Standard getmember('plugins') failed - this is why the workaround was needed\")\n                    standard_member = None\n\n                # The fragility is that if getmember() behavior changes between\n                # Python versions, the custom cache becomes a band-aid that\n                # masks the real issue\n                assert cached_member is not None, \"Custom cache should find directory\"\n            except Exception as e:\n                pytest.fail(f\"Directory lookup failed: {e}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    pytest.main([__file__, '-v'])\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":222,"oldLines":46,"newStart":222,"newLines":122,"lines":["             # becomes the only thing keeping extraction working. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01QssnQ5rwTFNSvVBSVBqihx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_013MLfACSNcrzo53ZEz3WrcR","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/regression_test.py","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-\n\"\"\"\nRegression test for Galaxy collection tar directory extraction fragility.\n\nThis test demonstrates the issue where the custom normalized cache in\n_extract_tar_dir creates fragile lookups when tar archives have directory\nentries that don't match the expected format.\n\nThe custom cache strips trailing path separators when building the index,\nwhich was a workaround for CPython bug 47231. On modern Python (3.11+),\nthis workaround is no longer necessary, yet the fragile custom index\napproach remains, creating potential issues.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport io\nimport json\nimport os\nimport tarfile\nimport tempfile\nimport pytest\nfrom unittest.mock import Mock, patch\n\n# Import the function we're testing\nfrom ansible.galaxy.collection import _extract_tar_dir\nfrom ansible.errors import AnsibleError\n\n\nclass TestTarDirectoryExtractionFragility:\n    \"\"\"Test suite for tar directory extraction issues.\"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.fixture\n    def temp_dir(self):\n        \"\"\"Create a temporary directory for test output.\"\"\"\n        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:\n            yield tmpdir\n\n    def test_extract_tar_dir_with_missing_cache_entry(self, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that _extract_tar_dir fails gracefully when cache entry is missing.\n\n        This demonstrates the fragility: if the normalized cache doesn't have\n        the expected entry, extraction fails with a generic error message that\n        doesn't help debug the issue.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Create a mock tar with an empty cache (simulates missing entry)\n        mock_tar = Mock()\n        mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {}  # Empty cache\n\n        # Try to extract a directory - should fail\n        b_dest = temp_dir.encode('utf-8')\n        with pytest.raises(AnsibleError) as exc_info:\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins', b_dest)\n\n        assert \"Unable to extract\" in str(exc_info.value)\n        print(\"Confirmed: Directory extraction fails when cache entry is missing\")\n\n    def test_extract_tar_dir_requires_normalized_cache(self, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that _extract_tar_dir depends on the normalized cache mechanism.\n\n        This test shows that the function is tied to a specific cache implementation\n        rather than using the standard tarfile API. This is the fragility:\n        if the cache isn't built correctly, extraction fails.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Create a mock tar member\n        mock_member = Mock()\n        mock_member.type = tarfile.REGTYPE  # Regular directory type\n        mock_member.isdir = Mock(return_value=True)\n        mock_member.linkname = None\n\n        # Create a mock tar with properly normalized cache\n        mock_tar = Mock()\n        mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {\n            'plugins': mock_member  # Cache has normalized key\n        }\n\n        # Mock os functions\n        with patch('os.path.isdir', return_value=False), \\\n             patch('os.makedirs'), \\\n             patch('os.mkdir'):\n            # This should succeed because cache has the entry\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))\n\n        # Verify the mock was called (cache lookup worked)\n        assert mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache['plugins'] == mock_member\n\n    def test_custom_cache_strips_trailing_separators(self, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that demonstrates how the custom cache strips trailing separators.\n\n        This is the core of the fragility: the cache uses a custom normalization\n        strategy (removesuffix) rather than relying on the standard tarfile API.\n        If tar member names vary in trailing separator usage, the custom cache\n        approach becomes error-prone.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Create test data showing the normalization strategy\n        test_cases = [\n            ('plugins', 'plugins'),      # No separator: stays same\n            ('plugins/', 'plugins'),      # With separator: gets stripped\n            ('plugins/modules', 'plugins/modules'),       # Nested, no sep: stays same\n            ('plugins/modules/', 'plugins/modules'),      # Nested, with sep: gets stripped\n        ]\n\n        for original, expected_key in test_cases:\n            # Simulate cache building like the code does\n            normalized_key = original.removesuffix(os.path.sep)\n            assert normalized_key == expected_key, \\\n                f\"Normalization failed for '{original}'\"\n            print(f\"'{original}' → '{normalized_key}' (fragility point)\")\n\n    def test_tar_extraction_requires_exact_cache_key_match(self, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that extraction requires exact cache key matching.\n\n        The fragility is that the cache lookup is exact - if the key passed to\n        _extract_tar_dir doesn't match the cache key exactly (after normalization),\n        the extraction fails. This is unlike the standard tarfile API which might\n        be more forgiving.\n        \"\"\"\n        mock_member = Mock()\n        mock_member.type = tarfile.REGTYPE\n\n        mock_tar = Mock()\n        # Cache has 'plugins' as key (after normalization)\n        mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {'plugins': mock_member}\n\n        # Test 1: Exact match after normalization works\n        with patch('os.path.isdir', return_value=False), \\\n             patch('os.makedirs'), \\\n             patch('os.mkdir'):\n            # Both resolve to 'plugins' after normalization\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins/', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))  # Also becomes 'plugins'\n\n        # Test 2: No match raises error\n        with pytest.raises(AnsibleError, match='Unable to extract'):\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'nonexistent', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))\n\n        print(\"Confirmed: Extraction depends on normalized cache keys matching exactly\")\n\n    def test_normalized_cache_is_fragile_workaround(self, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that demonstrates why the custom cache is a fragile workaround.\n\n        The cache was added to work around CPython bug 47231, which affected\n        tarfile.getmember() behavior. However, relying on a custom workaround\n        instead of fixing the root issue creates fragility:\n\n        1. The workaround only applies if built correctly\n        2. The workaround is version-dependent\n        3. The workaround creates a fragile dependency on normalization strategy\n        4. On Python 3.11+, the workaround is unnecessary\n        \"\"\"\n        mock_member = Mock()\n        mock_member.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n        mock_member.mode = 0o755\n\n        mock_tar = Mock()\n\n        # Scenario 1: Cache built correctly - works\n        mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {'plugins': mock_member}\n        with patch('os.path.isdir', return_value=False), \\\n             patch('os.makedirs'), \\\n             patch('os.mkdir'):\n            try:\n                _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))\n                print(\"Scenario 1: With proper cache - SUCCESS\")\n            except AnsibleError as e:\n                pytest.fail(f\"Should work with proper cache: {e}\")\n\n        # Scenario 2: Cache not built - fails\n        mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {}\n        with pytest.raises(AnsibleError, match='Unable to extract'):\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))\n        print(\"Scenario 2: Without cache - FAILS with generic error\")\n\n        # Scenario 3: Cache built with wrong normalization - fails\n        mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {'plugins/': mock_member}  # Wrong key format\n        with pytest.raises(AnsibleError, match='Unable to extract'):\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))\n        print(\"Scenario 3: With wrong cache key format - FAILS\")\n\n    def test_fragility_of_custom_cache_approach(self, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test demonstrating why the custom cache approach is fragile.\n\n        The issue is that:\n        1. The function depends on _ansible_normalized_cache being pre-built correctly\n        2. If the cache isn't built exactly right, extraction silently fails\n        3. The error message doesn't indicate the root cause (missing/wrong cache entry)\n        4. On Python 3.11+, standard getmember() would work without this complexity\n\n        This shows why removing the custom cache and using tarfile.getmember()\n        directly would be better - simpler, more reliable, and uses the standard API.\n        \"\"\"\n        mock_member = Mock()\n        mock_member.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n\n        # The fragility: the function can't work without this exact attribute\n        mock_tar = Mock()\n\n        # Test 1: Missing cache attribute\n        del_attr = object()\n        if hasattr(mock_tar, '_ansible_normalized_cache'):\n            # Save and delete the attribute\n            saved = mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache\n        mock_tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {}  # Empty\n\n        with pytest.raises(KeyError):  # KeyError from cache, not a useful AnsibleError\n            _extract_tar_dir(mock_tar, 'plugins', temp_dir.encode('utf-8'))\n\n        print(\"Fragility confirmed: Extraction fails with KeyError when cache is missing\")\n        print(\"Better approach: Use tarfile.getmember() directly without custom cache\")\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    pytest.main([__file__, '-v'])\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":9,"cache_creation_input_tokens":470,"cache_read_input_tokens":76467,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":470},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"067ee923-27fa-4745-ab55-9bdfc25edfd5","uuid":"db6c035d-22bb-4ed3-a0f9-0436882495b4"}

io.BytesIO(readme_content))\n\n    def test_tar_extraction_with_trailing_separator_directories(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test extraction when tar has directories with trailing separators.\n\n        This tests the standard tar format where directories are stored with\n        trailing separators. The normalized cache should handle this correctly.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        # Open the tar and manually test the cache\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # Build the normalized cache like the code does\n            tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # Try to extract directories as the code does in FILES.json processing\n            collection_path = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'extract_test_1')\n            os.makedirs(collection_path)\n\n            # Extract 'plugins' directory (no trailing separator in manifest)\n            # But tar has it as 'plugins/' (with trailing separator)\n            try:\n                _extract_tar_dir(tar, 'plugins', collection_path.encode('utf-8'))\n                assert os.path.isdir(os.path.join(collection_path, 'plugins'))\n            except AnsibleError as e:\n                pytest.fail(f\"Failed to extract directory with trailing separator: {e}\")\n\n    def test_tar_extraction_without_trailing_separator_directories(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test extraction when tar has directories WITHOUT trailing separators.\n\n        This tests a non-standard but possible tar layout. This is where the\n        fragility becomes apparent - the custom cache may fail or behave\n        unexpectedly with this format.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=False)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # Check what members are actually in the tar\n            members = {m.name for m in tar.getmembers()}\n            print(f\"Tar members: {members}\")\n\n            # Build the normalized cache like the code does\n            tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            print(f\"Normalized cache keys: {tar._ansible_normalized_cache.keys()}\")\n\n            # Try to extract directories\n            collection_path = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'extract_test_2')\n            os.makedirs(collection_path)\n\n            # Extract 'plugins' directory\n            # If tar has 'plugins' (no separator), cache should have 'plugins'\n            try:\n                _extract_tar_dir(tar, 'plugins', collection_path.encode('utf-8'))\n                assert os.path.isdir(os.path.join(collection_path, 'plugins'))\n            except AnsibleError as e:\n                pytest.fail(f\"Failed to extract directory without trailing separator: {e}\")\n\n    def test_normalized_cache_normalization_behavior(self, temp_tar_path):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that demonstrates the fragility: unnecessary normalization adds complexity.\n\n        This test shows that when tar archives store directories with trailing\n        separators (the standard), the custom normalized cache strips them during\n        lookup. This creates a point of fragility - if the tar member name format\n        changes, the cache might fail to find entries.\n\n        The normalization is unnecessary on Python 3.11+ where tarfile.getmember()\n        correctly handles directory lookups.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            members = {m.name for m in tar.getmembers()}\n\n            # In standard tar format, directories have trailing separators\n            # E.g., 'plugins/' and 'plugins/modules/'\n            dir_members = [m for m in tar.getmembers() if m.isdir()]\n            print(f\"Directory members in tar: {[m.name for m in dir_members]}\")\n\n            # Build the normalized cache like the code does\n            cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # Demonstrate the fragility: the cache has different keys than the tar members\n            # This means lookups depend on the normalization being correct\n            for m in dir_members:\n                normalized_key = m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep)\n                # If code is called with the normalized key, it works\n                assert normalized_key in cache, f\"Normalized key '{normalized_key}' should be in cache\"\n                # But if the tar member name format changes (e.g., no trailing sep),\n                # the relationship breaks\n                print(f\"Tar member '{m.name}' → cache key '{normalized_key}'\")\n\n            # The fragility: if getmember() behavior changed between Python versions\n            # (which it did - that's what bug 47231 was about), this workaround\n            # becomes the only thing keeping extraction working. But it's brittle\n            # because the normalization assumes all directory members have trailing seps.\n\n    def test_fragility_with_trailing_sep_mismatch(self, temp_tar_path, temp_dir):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that demonstrates the fragility of the custom cache with standard tar format.\n\n        In standard tar format, directories are stored with trailing separators.\n        The custom cache strips these, relying on the normalization working correctly.\n        This test shows that if the tar member format varies (some with, some without\n        trailing separators), the custom cache approach becomes fragile.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Create a tar with directories having trailing separators (standard format)\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='w:gz') as tar:\n            # Create manifest\n            manifest = {\n                'collection_info': {\n                    'namespace': 'test',\n                    'name': 'collection',\n                    'version': '1.0.0',\n                    'authors': ['Test'],\n                    'license': ['GPL-3.0-or-later'],\n                    'description': 'Test collection',\n                    'repository': None,\n                    'documentation': None,\n                    'homepage': None,\n                    'issues': None,\n                    'readme': None,\n                    'tags': [],\n                    'dependencies': {},\n                }\n            }\n            manifest_bytes = json.dumps(manifest).encode('utf-8')\n            manifest_info = tarfile.TarInfo(name='MANIFEST.json')\n            manifest_info.size = len(manifest_bytes)\n            tar.addfile(manifest_info, io.BytesIO(manifest_bytes))\n\n            # Create FILES.json\n            files_manifest = {\n                'files': [\n                    {'name': '.', 'ftype': 'dir', 'chksum_type': None, 'chksum_sha256': None, 'format': 1},\n                    {'name': 'plugins', 'ftype': 'dir', 'chksum_type': None, 'chksum_sha256': None, 'format': 1},\n                ]\n            }\n            files_bytes = json.dumps(files_manifest).encode('utf-8')\n            files_info = tarfile.TarInfo(name='FILES.json')\n            files_info.size = len(files_bytes)\n            tar.addfile(files_info, io.BytesIO(files_bytes))\n\n            # Add directory with trailing separator (standard tar format)\n            dir_info = tarfile.TarInfo(name='plugins/')\n            dir_info.type = tarfile.DIRTYPE\n            dir_info.mode = 0o755\n            tar.addfile(dir_info)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # Verify the tar has 'plugins/' with trailing separator\n            members = {m.name for m in tar.getmembers()}\n            assert 'plugins/' in members, \"Tar should have 'plugins/' with trailing separator\"\n\n            # Build the custom cache like the code does\n            tar._ansible_normalized_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # The cache converts 'plugins/' → 'plugins'\n            assert 'plugins' in tar._ansible_normalized_cache\n            cached_member = tar._ansible_normalized_cache['plugins']\n            assert cached_member.name == 'plugins/', \"Cache should point to 'plugins/' member\"\n\n            # Now test extraction - FILES.json says to extract 'plugins' (no separator)\n            # The custom cache normalizes this and finds 'plugins/'\n            collection_path = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'extract_test')\n            os.makedirs(collection_path)\n\n            try:\n                _extract_tar_dir(tar, 'plugins', collection_path.encode('utf-8'))\n                extracted_path = os.path.join(collection_path, 'plugins')\n                assert os.path.isdir(extracted_path), \"Directory should be extracted\"\n                print(f\"Successfully extracted directory despite tar/manifest mismatch\")\n            except AnsibleError as e:\n                pytest.fail(f\"Should be able to extract directory: {e}\")\n\n    def test_custom_cache_is_workaround_not_solution(self, temp_tar_path):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that demonstrates the custom cache is a Python-version-specific workaround.\n\n        The custom cache was added as a workaround for CPython bug 47231, which affected\n        how tarfile.getmember() handles directory name lookups. On Python 3.11+, this\n        bug is fixed, making the workaround unnecessary and just adding fragility.\n\n        This test shows that the workaround works, but only because it bypasses the\n        tarfile API rather than fixing the real issue.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.create_tar_with_directory_variations(temp_tar_path, include_trailing_sep=True)\n\n        with tarfile.open(temp_tar_path, mode='r:gz') as tar:\n            # The custom cache approach\n            custom_cache = {\n                m.name.removesuffix(os.path.sep): m for m in tar.getmembers()\n            }\n\n            # On Python 3.11+, getmember() should work fine for directory lookups\n            # But the custom cache was added for older Python versions where it didn't\n            try:\n                # Try to use standard getmember() API\n                member_via_api = tar.getmember('plugins')\n                print(f\"Standard API found 'plugins': {member_via_api.name}\")\n\n                # The custom cache achieves the same result\n                member_via_cache = custom_cache.get('plugins')\n                assert member_via_cache is not None\n                print(f\"Custom cache found 'plugins': {member_via_cache.name}\")\n\n                # Both work on Python 3.11+, but the custom cache adds complexity\n                # and fragility that isn't needed\n                print(\"Both approaches work on Python 3.11+, but custom cache adds unnecessary complexity\")\n            except KeyError:\n                print(\"Standard API fails - custom cache workaround would be needed on older Python\")\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    pytest.main([__file__, '-v'])\n"}}
